ParlaMint-AT_2003-10-23-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00035_d7e4471	Mrs Bundesminister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2003-10-23-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00035_d7e4487	I held this presidency between July 2002 and May this year. I can say that is a network of 13 states that work so to speak, interregionally across the world, for individual security. ParlaMint-AT_2003-10-23-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00035_d7e6730	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! The present poster of Bruno Kreisky explains that Kreisky was a refugee who fled from the National Socialists in 1938, sought political asylum in Copenhagen and said to the people there: If you send me back now, you'll deliver me to the people I just escaped. – I would like to advance this, because, as the Volkshilfe campaign says, no one leaves his home easily, no one likes to separate himself from his family and no one likes to be a volunteer refugee. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] Mr President. High house! With this presentation, the government coalition, I say ‘consciously’, continues its practice of adopting laws regardless of the constitution and international obligations of Austria. As a result, asylum law and federal care law such as that of the other 43 laws of a total of 62 previously challenged by the Constitutional Court will be repealed as unconstitutional. 43 of the 62 laws adopted so far have been repealed by the Constitutional Court! I would like to say, first of all, that it is good that this amendment to the Asylum Act was not decided before the summer after massive interventions by opposition and NGOs, so that it was still time for reflection, also time for a proper parliamentary procedure, including two expert hearings, which has made some improvement in the text. Regrettably, however, this delay in the decision of the Asylum Act has also resulted in the decision of the Supreme Court on federal care which has now been taken in a very negative way and has led to an enormous deterioration in the position of all those who have so far prevented the homelessness of asylum seekers to a large extent, namely the countries and NGOs. This is now being thanked in the way that they are fully agreed upon in retroactive terms for the care provided by them, which is a clear non-constitutional nature. It should also be noted at this point that 700 positive asylum certificates are issued annually – I stress: 700 positive asylum certificates annually! – not necessarily the act of generosity that one would expect from a country with an asylum tradition such as Austria. In contrast to that picture which is publicly transmitted, after which we are flooded by refugees, only 4.26 percent of the cases received asylum, a majority of the procedures, i.e. 83 percent, were discontinued. The law is so restrictive in its tendencies that it will make Austria an asylum country to the end in the European Union. Overall, it is characterised by more restrictions on access, less access to individual cases and less legal protection. In the view of UNHCR and all NGOs, the current asylum law would have been a suitable legal basis for resolving fair asylum procedures. However, the fact that in recent years, due to the increasing number of asylum applications, there have been shortcomings mainly due to the lack of resources of the asylum authorities, both in terms of personnel and in financial terms, has also confirmed this by the head of the Federal Asylamt in the hearing. He said that 36 additional officials employed at the Bundesasylamt have caused a substantial procedural acceleration and a reduction in the existing cases. Therefore, together with the NGOs, we believe that the acceleration and streamlining of procedures can only be achieved by increasing staff, and therefore in a few years, it will be shown that this law has by no means accelerated, but that – quite the contrary! – the federal constitution and the international obligations of Austria have been violated in this way. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] For some details: The restrictions on access to the Austrian territory have now been designed in such a way that de facto refugees can no longer come to Austria by land, unless by means of organized tractors or traffickers. Appeals against the first-instance decisions have suspensive effect in Austria. That is the standard that our rule of law gives us and that applies everywhere in our legal order. With your proposal, Mr Minister, the appeal should no longer have a suspensive effect, but the UBAS should only be able to recognise the suspensive effect in individual cases. This leaves the rule of law designated way and burdened the appeal authority with additional work – that authority whose task it is, in fact, to convince itself in a judicial manner of the credibility of an asylum seekers. This authority must now decide in a file procedure, without the possibility of personal contact with the asylum seekers, whether the appeal is hopeless or not. This means: rapid decision-making or growing of the file residues. The same applies to the ban on innovation. This reduces the possibilities for asylum seekers to present reasons not yet mentioned in the appeal procedure. This is an exclusive entry into Austria within the Member States of the European Union at the expense of persecuted people. As a result, they will not reduce the number of appeals, and as a consequence of this law, asylum procedures will not be shortened in the first and second instances, because this government template uses a dubious instrument which is clear from the outset that it will not stand the constitutional examination of the VfGH. For this purpose, the rule of law was sacrificed to the dictatorship of efficiency and sparingness, instead of the government, such as in Germany, simply increasing the state of staff of the Federal Office for Refugees. In Germany, 2 300 officials work around 60,000 cases, in Austria 200 to 300 employees in the Bundesasylamt and in the independent Bundesasylsenat 30,000 cases – half with a tenth of the staff stand! In the past, the support for asylum seekers has only worked half smoothly, because the countries and civil society have provided money and accommodation. Volkshilfe, Red Cross, Evangelic Diacony, Caritas: They all deserve public praise and recognition in truth! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] – You thank these organisations in your own way, namely by retroactively expropriating their claims under the Federal Service Act. You also admit this in the explanatory remarks when you say that certain disappointments of confidence – as you express it – can be linked to such retroactive effects of the clarifications made by this federal law. At the same time, the replacement rights of these organisations are completely destroyed and, therefore, this scheme is unconstitutional. This was also impressive in the expert hearing of the Constitutional Lawyer Heinz Mayer, as well as all other experts who spoke in the course of the review process: Professor Heinz Mayer: The repeal of the retroactive effect of the laws by means of authentic interpretation constitutes a massive intervention into property. The complete destruction as a result is certainly not proportionate and therefore unconstitutional. Dr. Köfner, UNHCR: The EU's ban on innovation is unique. Christian Neumayer, Vienna: It is curious that this presentation will be presented at the very time when there is negotiations with the countries. Andreas Lepchi, Caritas: There is a looser relationship with personal freedom, the idea of abuse and the fear dominate the bill. Heinz Patzelt: There are numerous constitutional and convention breaks. – He also criticises that although employment opportunities for asylum seekers are positive, there is no minimum labour law guarantee. Christoph Riedl criticises the concept of abuse and economic refugees. He says: The fact that with the Federal Service Act the claims are not met by the is, in fact, a “griff into the pockets of the donors. ” Thus, the Evangelical deacony had placed €900 000 in the accommodation of asylum seekers last year. Professor Theo Öhlinger: To be postponed during an appeal procedure is unconstitutional. Austria must be designated as a safe third country. Caritas: In particular, a serious and realistic acceleration of the first instance procedures at six to eight weeks would bring much better quality. The constitutional irregularities found by experts in this draft are legends! Today, the government coalition will adopt a law that is at the expense of the poorest of the poor, because through this whole draft law it is a reflection based on the assumption that asylum seekers are usually not refugees seeking protection from persecution, but people who want to find a comfortable life, that they are more economic refugees, and therefore that asylum procedures are to be carried out primarily for the purpose of ensuring the expulsion and not for the purpose of implementing an asylum procedure. Professor Heinz Mayer formulated this very, very strikingly before the summer in a “standard” interview. He explained that once again the effectiveness and efficiency of asylum procedures should be increased, but that the economy should not be self-evident, but that the state must first and foremost ensure that people do not use their interests in an unhindered manner, but orderly, as the obligations of international law, the constitution and the other legal rules offer. - That's it.

There is a principle of asylum right in the Geneva Convention on Refugees, according to which no one seeking protection from persecution can be pushed back to where he has fled because he is in need of protection from persecution. We all remember the Hungarian uprising, we remember the Czech crisis, we remember the situation when the Iron Curtain was directly on Austria's borders, that Austria has always acted exemplaryly, when refugees have come to Austria with open arms, in accordance with the Geneva Convention – those are people who have been persecuted either racially, religiously or politically. But, ladies and gentlemen, the situation has changed dramatically. We must not close our eyes before that! In 2002, 37 000 asylum applications were submitted in Austria. This is no longer comparable to the time when “convention refugees” actually came to Austria. That now has a different quality. You have to address things openly, too. If, in total, only, as the Federal Minister says, asylum status has actually been granted in 1,000 cases, then it must be said that 25 percent of these applicants are simply schinasylants, people who come to Austria and are not “convention refugees” but have other reasons. It may be social reasons, but these are mainly economic reasons. Before that, we must not shut down here. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! A word about the so-called refugee aid organisations. Some of them have a very questionable role – I deliberately address it. One should be able to address these things openly here in the House and be allowed to, one must also address them openly. Close You all eyes, or don't you know what you're doing, ladies and gentlemen? – Some of these organisations should be referred to as ‘immigration association’. [[Abg. Oils: Which one?]] I ask you, I ask the Caritas, I ask amnesty internationally: Read also Article 2 of the Geneva Convention on Refugees, which means – I quote – that: “Every refugee has obligations towards the country in which it is situated, including, in particular, the obligation to comply with the laws and other legislation and the measures taken to maintain public order.” – I hope that Article 2 will be read out too, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. Oillinger: What do you want to say with it?]] As part of this so-called hearing in Parliament, the experts have told us that the situation in Austria is dramatic, because there is nothing to beautify, it is dramatic. Three illegals come to Austria per hour! The fact is, ladies and gentlemen, that half of the so-called asylum seekers disappear immediately into illegality. The fact is that many of these people deliberately conceal their identity, no longer indicate their identity. The fact is that 350 people disappear into the illegality every week from the Traiskirchen camp alone – please, the mayor of Traiskirchen says. The fact is that 300 to 400 food is destroyed daily in the Traiskirchen camp, because 1 100 people are reported and 300 to 400 people are simply not present under non-compliance with the house rules. That is the reality, ladies and gentlemen. And in view of that, I ask you, in particular, about the opposition: How honest can it be that a so-called asylum-seeker who first comes across the border illegally and then immediately dives? Is he really in need of protection? Does he show that he needs protection when he dives immediately? How honest is it if he makes an asylum application in Austria and then immediately travels to another country? How in need of protection is he really? – He does not come to Austria to seek protection, but he immediately travels to another country. [[Abg. Gaál: These are exceptions!]] How honest it is when someone always invents new reasons why he believes he is a refugee, my dear Ladies and gentlemen? . We mean, asylum seekers can be demanded of honesty. Now I come to a point that so-called good people here in this House of course do not like to hear, namely, the connection between crime and schinasylant. That's what you should come to talk about. The experts confirm that, that's not our opinion, it's experts who call numbers. The figures confirm the following: Ladies and gentlemen: Of 1,229 Nigerians – mainly due to addiction and violent offences – there are 790 asylum seekers. Of 1,700 Armenians displayed, 680 are asylum seekers. These are figures before which we must not close our eyes! They point out that some of the people here who are illegally resident and those who believe they can apply for asylum here fall into crime. [[Abg. Oils: Why do they fall into crime?]] Do you know what the experts say? – In some areas, up to 50 percent of the asylum applicants shown are asylum seekers. In order to avoid a deportation, chain applications are then made. There are always new reasons why you think you still get asylum in Austria. The experts' statements are probably clear, as they say, literally: It is less and less possible to bring a foreign lawbreaker outside of the country. Ladies and gentlemen! There's really a need for action. There is a need for action when the crime rate explodes in Austria. At the KSÖ conference last week in Leogang, experts were told that the situation in Eastern Austria is marked by a dramatic increase in the number of raids happening in Vienna alone in one day than in Salzburg in a whole year! Experts say: A cloud of illegals roams across Europe. This is not our statement, but experts say this. There is action to be taken, and this is the task of the House! One contribution to this is, of course, this new asylum law, which is a law for real asylum cases. We want to continue to live up to our role as an asylum country. Persons according to the Geneva Convention on Refugees, i.e. refugees, are admitted by us Austrians with open arms and care. But the law should also be a signal that Austria is not a country of immigration, not even for those who give false information that simply subvert or even commit crimes in Austria!

ParlaMint-AT_2003-10-23-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00035_d7e9255	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, As an asylum country, Austria looks back on a long tradition and can be proud of it. Our country has opened its borders to refugees again and again generously. [[Abg. May. Worm: Until you've come!]] For example, we recorded hundreds of thousands of Hungarians and Czechs when they had to leave their home country. We have also granted more than 90 000 Bosnians asylum during the Bosnia-Herzegovina war and have integrated 60 000 of them into our society. No other European country can refer to such generosity in asylum policy. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Ladies and gentlemen! ==References====External links== World Congress of the AWR, the research society for the world refugee problem, honoured Austria as a very good country of asylum. At this conference, the representative of UNHCR Germany also pointed out that harmonisation of asylum laws in the European Union is therefore so difficult because there are countries such as Germany and Austria that have very high standards, and many other countries with significantly lower standards. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! On the criticism of the UNHCR, the world headquarters from Geneva, one has to say that it came at a time when the amendment to this law we are discussing today was not even designed! – Dear Ones [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Niederwieser.]] Ladies and gentlemen of the opposition, please stop talking our country, our home country Austria, constantly bad! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] [[Intervocations with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] Despite many other concerns, we want to maintain the high standard of Austria as a good country of asylum and continue to offer very good conditions for asylum seekers who actually need asylum. However, we want to push back on asylum abuse. With the new asylum law, we are trying to take into account in a balanced way both the interests of our citizens and the interests of the asylum seekers who actually need asylum. It can be neither in the sense of our Austrian fellow citizens nor in the interest of those seeking help and those who are persecuted, that Austria will become more target country number one of the international tow bands that drag people to Austria, which is awaiting economic improvement here. The figures, ladies and gentlemen, speak for themselves: 85 percent of asylum seekers in Austria cannot provide sufficient asylum grounds. In the EU, according to UN High Commissioner for Refugees, Lubbers cannot even provide a solid asylum ground for the asylum seekers. – It is also urgently necessary to speed up the procedures. The negative votes that we have heard today about the ban on innovation can be resisted: Awesome procedures are in the sense of those who need help! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Besides, my dear ones. Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to point out once again – and the critics constantly overlook this – that in special cases the new law provides for exceptions and provides for second instance innovations. So there is no ban on innovation, for example, if procedures at first instance were inadequate. And further exceptions are made if facts or evidence become available to the applicant only later. Similarly, victims of torture or traumatised are excluded from this scheme. To improve family procedures: If a family member is granted asylum, this applies to the whole family. The legal protection of asylum seekers through the acquisition of legal assistance is another achievement of this innovation. Finally, we can say that this law takes into account harmonisation at European level and international standards. The requirements of the Geneva Convention on Refugees, as well as the European Convention on Human Rights, are met by this law. A look over our borders shows that this proposal is in line with other EU countries' asylum laws, such as Germany or Holland. In the EU comparison, Austria is at the forefront of asylum applications: In Austria, 4.6 asylum seekers – which last year were 39 345 applications – come to 1,000 inhabitants; in Germany there are 0.87, in Great Britain 0.18, in Italy 0.1 – or in absolute numbers: 7,700, and in a country six times the size of Austria. Ladies and gentlemen! Federal Minister Strasser has proposed important and long-term overdue measures in the revision of the law. The new law allows quick help to those they need. The allegations that it is not in conformity with the Constitutional Service of the Federal Chancellery, as well as those of renowned lawyers, such as Professor Gruber or Professor Matscher, can be opposed. Ladies and gentlemen! The new asylum law will therefore be another part of the history of our country as a successful, very good and safe country of asylum.

ParlaMint-AT_2003-10-23-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00035_d7e18377	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! I would like to focus briefly on two issues: the Alpine Convention and the second of the International Criminal Court. In connection with the Alpine Convention, I am pleased that I am on the 24th. In June of this year, the agreement was signed in the presence of Mayor Hilde Zach in Innsbruck, in the Golden Dachl, where the office is located. I would also like to tell you why I was happy about this: With this, Austria has brought an international organization back to Austria, and this time even into a state capital – something that Mr Hakl has also mentioned today and felt important. I'll do that, too. We have a vital interest in the protection and sustainable development of this most important ecosystem in Europe, namely our Alps. Since the 1980s, we have tried to move forward with the Alpine Convention. Finally, it was adopted under the Austrian Presidency in 1991. We have ratified as one of the first countries, namely in 1995. But it took until 2003 until it was signed by all the other Alpine Convention members. I can say that we are now focusing on a considerable number of issues: Tourism, energy, nature conservation, spatial planning, mountain agriculture and transport. Of course, this variety of topics also reflects the complexity of life in a state whose territory – and we must always look at it – consists of nearly two thirds of mountains. At the same time, this holistic approach of the Alpine Convention and its protocols makes it one of the most important instruments for the sustainable development of an entire mountain region. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] We have also been actively involved, ladies and gentlemen, in the negotiation of nine protocols to the Alpine Convention over the years, and at the heart of these efforts – and this has already been mentioned in the discussion today – was, of course, the protocol on transport, which has now been concluded by a protocol to resolve disputes in order to ensure a sustainable transport policy in the Alpine region in the future. The central element is the abandonment of new high-level roads for cross-country traffic. Ladies and gentlemen! Finally, Austria's efforts were completed at the seventh Alpine Conference, which took place in Merano in November 2002. It is a great success. There was the possibility of an exchange, for the time being a French Secretary-General, but it was based in Austria. I think that was a good exchange, because the seat is secured for many years. I hope that the effect that comes from this seat and from this Alpine Convention in Tyrol itself will be radiated, also into Bolzano, where there is an outside office of the Secretariat. Now a brief comment on an item on the agenda, which seems to me particularly important, namely to the International Criminal Court. Hardly any other international decision has been discussed as intensively and as long as the establishment of this criminal court. Meanwhile, 139 states are already parties to the Roman Statute, and I think that is already very important. The International Criminal Court is to exercise jurisdiction over the most serious international crimes, i.e. genocide, crimes against humanity and war crimes. I mean, to be able to say that we have actually arrived in a new era of international law. The European Union and Austria have supported the establishment of this Court of Justice from the very beginning, and of course we are also working together to promote universal ratification of the Rome Statute and to strengthen and improve the effectiveness of the Court of Justice. Mrs Lunacek! We therefore regret, together with our colleagues from the EU, the action of the United States of America to conclude now bilateral agreements based on Article 98 of the Rome Statute aimed at excluding the transfer of US citizens to this criminal court. On this issue – and you know that – the European Union has already adopted directives in September 2002, which we have fully contributed and supported. I very often refer to these directives in my external contacts. The European Union also regularly informs States concerned about this common attitude in the form of demarches. So here, of course, we stay on it. Let me conclude by saying that the legal status of the Court of Justice itself, its institutions and other persons involved in the proceedings before the Court of Justice is at last fully regulated in this Convention. The ratification of this Convention will bring this international regime into force for Austria and thus ensure the functioning of this Court of Justice also in Austria. - Thank you.

Genocide, crimes against humanity, war crimes, crimes of the attack war have the 20th. ==References====External links== The war criminal tribunal of Leipzig in 1921 and the military tribunals of Nuremberg and Tokyo after the Second World War have shown that the industrialization of the war and the instrumentalization of people to commit war crimes require a new form of prosecution of rulers, dictators and warlords. ParlaMint-AT_2003-10-23-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00035_d7e18634	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen in the Chambers of Deputies! I should like to express a personal opinion on the comments made by Mr Lichtenberger. I am really sorry that you are never satisfied with what is being done, that you do not appreciate the work, that you speak of “offering” et cetera, that you cannot perceive or see things. I personally believe that our foreign policy is really very, very sustainablely planned, implemented, implemented, and I am actually very, very proud of what can be presented in this country on the subject of foreign policy. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The most important event this year was, of course, the enlargement of the EU. It has been one of the great priorities of Austria, and we have done this very, very well. Ten candidate countries in Eastern and Southern Europe could now be included in the ranks, and the European Council in Copenhagen about a year ago managed to complete the last open chapters. This was with Estonia, Latvia, Lithuania, Malta, Poland, Slovakia, Slovenia, the Czech Republic, Hungary and Cyprus. ==References== It will then be so far in May 2004. The EU will then cover a total of 25 Member States with over 450 million citizens. In addition to creating this united continent, the aim of this EU enlargement is to define common values such as political stability, such as peace and, of course, respect for human rights. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] In addition, of course, there is another point: It will be the largest single market in the world. However, half the way to success is good preparation, and since you know that in Austria and also our neighbours we need to prepare a lot more, we still need to work on it intensively. While the common objective, the development of an economically, socially and culturally integrated border area, remains the same, of course, there will need to be some change and change in the institutional area. This is why facilitating cross-border cooperation between local authorities, whether the federal, state or local authorities, is of enormous importance. The present Additional Protocol on this cross-border cooperation between local authorities will now provide the legal basis to facilitate this cooperation. Austria has the framework agreement on 28 March. It was signed on 1 February 2001 subject to ratification. This has taken place, and now this right applies also to Austria. How did this additional protocol come about? – The main obstacle to the effective application of the existing Framework Convention, which has been in force for 20 years, was precisely this lack of legality, which was not the case in the measures implemented by the local authorities. The Committee of Ministers therefore decided in 1995 to have this additional protocol drawn up. This is precisely about this cross-border cooperation, which has not only changed since the case of the Iron Curtain, but has become extremely important. This Additional Protocol now removes the legal obstacles to these joint projects. In summary, this Additional Protocol will significantly simplify and improve this cross-border cooperation in our regions of Europe. Personally, I am very glad that all the groups of the House are taking part here and recognizing the importance of these laws.

[[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] I have already said it in the committee – which, although Mrs Stoisits has stimulated to object, I say it now and take the objection into account because I am convinced of it –: I think it is a pleasure to be able to agree to this law, and I also think I know that all four political groups will agree. On the one hand, it simplifies access to legislation for public authorities, but certainly also for citizens, because anyone who has an Internet connection can come very easily to legislation in the future. Whether it's him or then will be done or not, one will see, but he can in any case provide a good overview of the legislation. The second aspect – which is also pleasing – is the savings law: For the federal government alone, savings will be made in the year of €400 000, and I think that is actually a very remarkable sum. By the way, however, I would like to highlight very positively the initiative of my colleague, Dr Wittmann, who has made some proposals for improvement in the run-up to the Committee's deliberations. The most important thing is, of course, a provision for the failure of the system. What happens when the electronic system collapses? – For this purpose, the draft law provides for a corresponding provision: that there are certified copies, which are withdrawn and also certified and collected from the various bodies, where consultation can then be taken. That is to say, it is foreseen that nothing can happen in the event of the system's failure. In connection with this law, which, in principle, I have said, gives rise to joy in the first place, but I would like to stress the RIS, the legal information system of the federal government. I would also like to thank the Members who are working on it very much at this point. It is a very good system, you can work well with it, you have a good overview. They have also developed it further, they have done a lot of work in relation to this legal information system and, I think, it has been very user-friendly. Thank you very much. I hope and would be pleased that all political groups will agree to this milestone in the procurement system. Thank you.

Point report of the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection on the Government template (134 d.a._Other Organiser Protocol to the 1979 Convention on Long-range Transboundary Air Pollution on Heavy Metals and Declarations (239 D.B.)
ParlaMint-AT_2003-10-23-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00035_d7e20597	We're now on the 9th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. As the first speaker, Mr Hornek spoke. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2003-10-23-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00035_d7e20623	Mr President. Federal Minister! Honourable Members! High house! Environmental policy requires international cooperation in order to achieve sustainable effects. Austria has not only tried to achieve particularly close cooperation in the environmental field at the pan-European level because of its geographical situation, but has also played an active role in the development of international mandatory standards for air pollution in Europe in the past. The Convention on Long-Range Transboundary Air Pollution, drawn up in the framework of the UN Economic Commission for Europe and concluded by 33 States and the Commission, was one of the first and most important regional agreements on the environment in Europe. The objective of reducing the cross-border exposure to air pollution in the European framework has been defined by a number of protocols to the Convention. The current heavy metals protocol, which deals with cross-border pollutants of lead, mercury and cadmium, will take another step against environmental pollution. This is another of the many measures implemented by Austria in the international context under this Protocol. Austria has already fulfilled all the essential obligations of the heavy metal protocol. There is therefore no need to act with regard to new limit values, new product restrictions or new regulations. The production of emission balances required in the Protocol has already been carried out in excellent work by the Federal Environment Agency for many years. However, for Austria, this Protocol is of enormous importance. Since the Alps act as a blatant slope of heavy metals, which are irrigated by the air currents, pollution is particularly visible in the global area. Mercury is a special problem here, as it is easily imitated and transported far due to its physical structure. In this respect, the implementation of the Protocol and the mandatory restrictions such as the lead content of petrol, the mercury content of batteries and recommended measures in fluorescent lamps, paints et cetera will lead to a global reduction in emissions and in the long term reduce the environmental burden of the Alps in particular. The inhabitants of the border regions along the Iron Curtain have had to experience painfully over the last decades, meaning cross-border air pollution cargo. In the past, there has also been significant damage to the environment in my home region, the forest district, caused by imported air pollutants and resulting in a negative impact on the environment. It is all the more gratifying that in recent years advanced environmental standards have been and are being adopted by our neighbouring countries and that this has led to noticeable improvements in air quality. Many environmental activities, most of which were initiated in the forest district, have contributed in partnership with our northern neighbours. These include concepts for energy saving and the use of renewable energy sources such as biomass and solar energy. Many of these projects have also been implemented with Austrian technology, for the environmental benefit of both sides. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The recognition that the protection and conservation of the environment is only possible through a paradigm change of social and economic de velopment. Our former Minister for Agriculture of the ÖVP and Vice President Josef Riegler recognized development more than a decade ago and made the path of the eco-social market economy the concept of sustainability policy. Under the term "sustainability" or "sustainable development", we see today the solution to create and maintain fair living conditions, even disadvantaged regions and subsequent generations. The implementation of this Protocol Heavy metals is one of many steps on the right path. This is a way to secure steps and with political weight through our Environment Minister Dipl.-Ing. Josef Pröll. – Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-AT_2003-10-23-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00035_d7e20696	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! My contribution relates to the Government template for the 1979 Convention on Long-range Transboundary Air Pollution on Heavy Metals. Ladies and gentlemen! This Convention shall require each Contracting Party to comply without delay with the strategies and programmes set out in these Protocols. The Parties have therefore decided to implement the Convention on Long-range Transboundary Air Pollution. The Parties are concerned that emissions from certain heavy metals, such as cadmium, lead or mercury, may extend beyond natural limits, cause damage to ecosystems with an environmental and economic significance and have harmful effects on human health. However, a Party may also take measures to develop and use clean procedures to prevent and limit pollution. One example is the use of renewable energies such as wind power. This alternative is currently booming in Austria. By the end of the year, 300 windwheels will supply 220 000 households with electricity. Some €300 million will be invested in wind power projects this year. Wind power has already become an important economic factor in Austria. There are 2 300 employees through the use of wind power at Austrian companies and suppliers. Above all, the federal states of Lower Austria and Burgenland have taken over the pioneering role here. Ladies and gentlemen! In June 1998, Austria signed the Heavy Metal Protocol to the Convention on Long-range Transboundary Air Pollution. It therefore has to be clearly more.

ParlaMint-AT_2003-10-23-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00035_d7e20887	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! As a final speaker, I too can deal with the so-called heavy metal protocol. In 1999, as my previous speakers have already explained, this heavy metal protocol to the Convention on Long-range Transboundary Air Pollution of the United Nations Economic Commission for Europe was adopted. Contracting States are 40 European states, the EU, the USA and Canada. Since then, eight protocols, a financing protocol and seven air re-content protocols have been drawn up on the basis of this Convention. Austria has ratified six of these so far. These air-re Content Protocols combat the extensive cross-border air pollution across Europe, as has already been said. Ladies and gentlemen! Although this heavy metal protocol is clearly worth noting, it accelerates the achievement of the same environmental standards in Europe. In particular, it also contributes significantly – and this has already been said – that Central and Eastern European countries are faster to bring their environmental standards into line with EU standards. For Austria, the heavy metal protocol does not mean a strengthening of existing legal provisions, as we already fulfil its essential obligations. However, an improvement in the environmental situation is expected with regard to cross-border emissions from our neighbouring countries. In the transport sector in particular, however, we certainly have a lot to do with. Transport development in recent decades has increased sharply in both freight and individual transport and has continued to show an increasing trend. The German Government will have the opportunity to accept this proof of truth by the next protocol to be implemented, the multi-component agreement. Thank you.
